# QA review charter — round 8: one question about grain

You are a fresh-context reviewer. You have full repo access. Read the
repo yourself; do not trust any summary in this charter over the bytes.

This module validates authored workflow documents against a DECLARED
schema. A load-time gate checks the declaration is closed; an interpreter
applies it to a document; a normalizer derives the admitted form.

The interpreter keeps PER-RUN STATE while it walks: which positions have
been evaluated, which produced a usable value, which named obligations
are still outstanding, what each derivation step needs. Until the change
under review, some of that state was keyed by the DECLARATION — a key
every instance of that declaration shares — so one instance's outcome
silently decided another's. It has been re-keyed to the INSTANCE address.

**This round asks ONE question, and nothing else:**

> **Does any per-run state still live at a COARSER GRAIN than the thing
> it describes?**

Reliability, completion, outstanding obligations, effective configs, hook
bookkeeping, memoisation, tag status — any of it. A key that is shared by
two things that are not the same thing is the defect.

## 0. READ THIS FIRST — the time budget

**You have a HARD 20-minute wall-clock budget, enforced by an external
process kill. A session that hits it produces NO usable output — every
finding you have not yet written out is discarded.** An earlier session in
this repo spent its budget investigating, planned to write up at the end,
and was killed mid-write-up; all of its work was lost.

**Write each finding out IN FULL, in the §4 format, the moment you
confirm it.** Stop investigating around the 12-minute mark. Breadth at
shallow depth; anything costing over two minutes goes down as `unrun`
with one sentence on what would settle it.

## 1. The review object

| File | Lines | sha256 (first 16) |
|---|---|---|
| `v3/src/definition/schema/engine.ts` | 1676 | `ed338f5147a09001` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/defineSurface.ts` | 727 | `5b33eb6481da48a9` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/normalizer.ts` | 130 | `a9a7a8a67d7d8f62` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/vocabulary.ts` | 454 | `760feff342744abe` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/templateFormat.ts` | 574 | `69719e9fc9196e14` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/engine.test.ts` | 1495 | `579c67ef15d57fec` |

The commit under review is the most recent, whose subject begins
`fix(v3): per-instance bookkeeping`. The round it answers is
`v3/implementation/ch13-rederivation-arm/p3-aftermath/arm-round7-out.txt`.
Both are worth reading first.

Both modules are importable, so a declaration can be built, passed to the
gate, and run through the interpreter in a few lines. Use that.

## 2. What counts, and what does not

**A finding is: two distinct things sharing one key in per-run state, with
an input where that sharing changes the answer.** The input matters — a
shared key nothing can distinguish is a tidiness observation, not a
defect, and should be marked as such.

Worth attention because they are where sharing hides: several instances
of one declaration (open-map entries, list members, repeated bindings);
one instance reached by two routes (a value class referenced from two
sites, a union and its map case, aliased YAML nodes that are the same
object in two places); positions whose reporting path is deliberately
COARSER than their address (`memberLaneAt: "container"`,
`keyLaneAt: "container"`, a membership rule declaring `at: "container"`);
the two channels; and anything keyed by a rendered string rather than a
structural address.

Out of scope, recorded as CARRIED-SCOPE with no proposed fix: the gate
refusing something the interpreter would have handled; message wording;
performance; naming taste; the declaration file's lack of a ratification
byte-lock until the next phase.

## 3. The lenses

**Lens A — enumerate the state.** Read the interpreter's run state and
list EVERY map, set and array it keeps during a walk. For each: what is
the key, what is the thing it describes, and are those the same
granularity? Say so for each one, including the ones that are fine — a
list that reports only problems does not tell the reader what was
checked.

**Lens B — try to make two things collide.** For each state item, build a
declaration and a document where two distinct positions would produce the
same key, and check whether one's outcome changes the other's. The
instance address is a list of segments; ask what it does NOT distinguish.
Consider a key that is a number and one that is the string of that
number; keys containing the separator the encoding uses; a document where
the same object appears at two addresses.

**Lens C — the three claims of the commit.** Check rather than believe:
one open-map entry can no longer decide another's rule; a refused
delegation reports and is silent only when a sibling lane failed; a
`default:` outside a fixed map's field is refused at load.

**Lens D — regression.** Run and report `pnpm v3:typecheck`,
`pnpm v3:lint`, `pnpm v3:test`, `bash v3/adr/check.sh`, and confirm the
live declaration passes the gate. **Run the suite ALONE — no concurrent
test runs.** One test, `runnerJourney` / `J-RESPAWN`, is a known
pre-existing intermittent failure unrelated to this module, reproduced on
earlier commits; if you see it, say so and move on.

## 4. Evidence bar and finding format

**Every finding must carry an executed command and its real output**, or
be marked `unrun`. Work under `/tmp`; do not modify the repository.

```
### F<n> — <one-line title>
CLASS: IN-SCOPE | CARRIED-SCOPE | UNRUN
LENS:  A | B | C | D
WHAT:  the defect in one sentence
WHERE: file:line
EVIDENCE:
  <exact command>
  <exact output>
WHY IT MATTERS: what a maintainer concludes wrongly
FIX SKETCH: (IN-SCOPE only) the smallest correction
```

## 5. Rules of engagement

- **Do not modify the repository.** A byte guard runs before and after.
- **Do not propose new machinery.**
- **Proportionality.** A correction larger than the thing it corrects is
  itself a finding.
- **Vocabulary.** Routine software quality review of an internal
  data-validation module — a configuration gate, the interpreter that
  consumes it, and a derivation step. Ordinary engineering terms
  throughout.
- **A clean result is a real result.** Do not manufacture findings and do
  not soften a real one.

## 6. Closing message

Only this:

- your tree's HEAD;
- counts: IN-SCOPE / CARRIED-SCOPE / UNRUN;
- the state items you enumerated, each marked matched-grain or coarser;
- the four commands and their real results;
- and the question, answered directly:

  > **Does any per-run state still live at a coarser grain than the thing
  > it describes — and if so, which, and what input shows it?**

# QA review charter — round 7: the closing question

You are a fresh-context reviewer. You have full repo access. Read the
repo yourself; do not trust any summary in this charter over the bytes.

This module validates authored workflow documents against a DECLARED
schema. A load-time gate checks that the declaration is closed; an
interpreter applies it; a separately-named normalizer derives the
admitted form from it.

Six review rounds have chased ONE failure mode through this substrate: a
declared rule that does not run, and leaves no trace anywhere that it did
not. Each round found another road to it and each was closed. The owner's
standing claim after the three changes under review is:

> **No known way remains for a declared rule to fail to run silently.**

**Your job is to falsify that claim.** Not to confirm it, and not to
review the changes for their own sake.

## 0. READ THIS FIRST — the time budget

**You have a HARD 20-minute wall-clock budget, enforced by an external
process kill. A session that hits it produces NO usable output — every
finding you have not yet written out is discarded.** An earlier session in
this repo spent its budget investigating, planned to write up at the end,
and was killed mid-write-up; all of its work was lost.

**Write each finding out IN FULL, in the §4 format, the moment you
confirm it.** Stop investigating around the 12-minute mark. Breadth at
shallow depth; anything costing over two minutes goes down as `unrun`
with one sentence on what would settle it.

## 1. The review object

| File | Lines | sha256 (first 16) |
|---|---|---|
| `v3/src/definition/schema/engine.ts` | 1556 | `0eea46898fdb87ca` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/defineSurface.ts` | 716 | `bc53d21af59e9561` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/normalizer.ts` | 122 | `ff232bf87f0b97bf` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/vocabulary.ts` | 454 | `760feff342744abe` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/templateFormat.ts` | 574 | `69719e9fc9196e14` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/engine.test.ts` | 1398 | `f526f2259ccbf13b` |

The three commits under review are the most recent whose subjects begin
`fix(v3): the third reader`, `feat(v3): an undecided rule`, and
`feat(v3): no value reaches`. The round they answer is
`arm-round6-out.txt` in this directory. Both are worth reading first.

Both modules are importable, so a declaration can be built, passed to the
gate, and run through the interpreter in a few lines. Use that.

## 2. What "silently" means here, exactly

A declared rule may legitimately not run. What it may NOT do is leave no
trace. Three dispositions are ACCEPTABLE and are not findings:

- the rule ran and produced a finding, or ran and passed;
- the rule was SUPPRESSED by an operand that itself failed — there is a
  sibling finding naming that failure, and that is the trace;
- the rule was refused at LOAD, so the declaration never became a surface.

One disposition is the defect: the rule did not run, produced nothing,
and nothing else in the output or at load records the fact.

Out of scope, recorded as CARRIED-SCOPE with no proposed fix: the gate
refusing something the interpreter would have handled (the loud
direction, several known cases deliberately open); message wording;
performance; naming taste; the recorded decision that the declaration
file carries no ratification byte-lock until the next phase.

## 3. The lenses

**Lens A — the three changes, checked rather than believed.**

1. The normalizer's operand paths now resolve through the interpreter's
   own descent rather than a reader of its own. Is there any declared
   path shape the gate accepts for which a hook still writes nothing?
   Wildcards in either hook, at any position; a path landing on
   something that is not the shape the hook expects; a hook whose target
   field collides with an object internal.
2. A rule still undecided at end of walk is reported, unless the
   declaration carries `whenOperandAbsent: "skip"`. Find a way for a
   rule to end the walk neither decided, nor suppressed with a trace,
   nor reported. Consider especially: rules reached through value
   classes, rules inside list members, the deferred queue's own
   re-entry, and whether the marker can be set where it should not be.
3. A declared `default:` is now evaluated through its own field. Does
   every path that materializes one go through that evaluation? What
   about a default on a node inside an open-map entry, a list member, a
   union case, a value class?

**Lens B — the roads no round has walked.** This is where a falsifier
should spend most of its budget. Take the interpreter's evaluation code
as the source of truth for when a rule RUNS, and enumerate every point
where it can return early, skip, continue, or decline. For each, ask: if
a declared rule sits below this point, what records that it did not run?
Places worth reading: container preconditions; `gating` and `dependsOn`
suppression; the open-map key-subset CONTINUE; a list member that
produced a finding; channel-scoped fields on each of the two channels; a
delegate whose registry is absent or whose registration declines; the
root container lane.

**Lens C — the load gate's own coverage.** The gate refuses what it can
prove wrong at load. Is there a declaration it accepts whose rules cannot
all run for some legal document — where the gate had enough information
to know? And the converse trace: does anything the gate refuses get
refused TWICE, or with a message naming the wrong thing?

**Lens D — regression.** Run and report `pnpm v3:typecheck`,
`pnpm v3:lint`, `pnpm v3:test`, `bash v3/adr/check.sh`, and confirm the
live declaration passes the gate. **Run the suite ALONE — no concurrent
test runs — and expect it to take about half a minute.** One test,
`runnerJourney` / `J-RESPAWN`, is a known pre-existing intermittent
failure unrelated to this module and confirmed on earlier commits; if you
see it, say so and move on.

## 4. Evidence bar and finding format

**Every finding must carry an executed command and its real output**, or
be marked `unrun`. Work under `/tmp`; do not modify the repository.

```
### F<n> — <one-line title>
CLASS: IN-SCOPE | CARRIED-SCOPE | UNRUN
LENS:  A1 | A2 | A3 | B | C | D
WHAT:  the defect in one sentence
WHERE: file:line
EVIDENCE:
  <exact command>
  <exact output>
WHY IT MATTERS: what a maintainer concludes wrongly
FIX SKETCH: (IN-SCOPE only) the smallest correction
```

## 5. Rules of engagement

- **Do not modify the repository.** A byte guard runs before and after.
- **Do not propose new machinery.**
- **Proportionality.** A correction larger than the thing it corrects is
  itself a finding.
- **Vocabulary.** Routine software quality review of an internal
  data-validation module — a configuration gate, the interpreter that
  consumes its output, and a derivation step. Ordinary engineering terms
  throughout.
- **A clean result is a real result.** Six rounds have narrowed this;
  finding nothing is a plausible honest outcome, and saying so is worth
  as much as a finding. Do not manufacture one, and do not soften a real
  one.

## 6. Closing message

Only this:

- your tree's HEAD;
- counts: IN-SCOPE / CARRIED-SCOPE / UNRUN;
- which lenses you completed, partially ran, or did not reach;
- the four commands and their real results;
- and then the closing question, answered directly:

  > **Can a declared rule still fail to run and leave no trace — and if
  > so, by which road?**

c24c65e5 fix(v3): per-instance bookkeeping — state keyed by the thing it describes
 v3/src/definition/schema/defineSurface.ts |  11 ++
 v3/src/definition/schema/engine.test.ts   |  99 +++++++++++-

commit c24c65e5f1e22857992c834acdc448b89790f972
Author:     Gergely Hodicska <gergely.hodicska@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Fri Aug 7 14:37:54 2026 +0200
Commit:     Gergely Hodicska <gergely.hodicska@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Fri Aug 7 14:37:54 2026 +0200

    fix(v3): per-instance bookkeeping — state keyed by the thing it describes
    
    The third member of the unification family, after one view of the
    document and one resolver for addresses.
    
    The engine's per-run state was keyed by the DECLARATION PATH, which every
    instance of that declaration shares. So one open-map entry's broken field
    decided another entry's rule, and the earlier `dependsOn` fix had papered
    over exactly one consumer of the same coarse key. State is now keyed by
    the INSTANCE address — the segment list the walk already carries, which
    is neither the finding path (a declared grain can coarsen it) nor the
    declaration path (every instance shares it).
    
    Round 7's F4 closes BY CONSTRUCTION, and the probe that exposed it is
    kept verbatim as the guard: entry A reports its own undecided lane, and
    adding a DIFFERENT, broken entry B no longer makes A's report vanish.
    
    F1 closes with an OBLIGATION that travels per instance to the end of the
    walk. A delegation the registry refuses is undischarged, not satisfied:
    the config was never validated and does not reach the admitted value, and
    that is now said out loud through the substrate's internal-failure branch
    — unless a sibling lane failed and IS the trace, which is the measured
    shape and is tested as the discriminating negative.
    
    THE SPECIAL CASE IS REDUNDANT AND IS DELETED, as the act required
    verifying: the sibling map threaded through six functions is gone, and
    its own guard — a valid entry reporting whether it comes before or after
    a broken sibling, plus the negative that the broken entry's dependent
    lane is still suppressed — passes on the general mechanism. Keeping both
    would have been a second model of one thing.
    
    Two riders, both ending a pass-and-ignore:
      · a `default:` outside a field of a map.fixed is REFUSED at load. That
        is the built answer to F2: absence is only detectable where a fixed
        map declares a key, so a default anywhere else has nothing to fill.
      · `expandAdvancesRound` reads EVERY position its operand path reaches,
        not the first. A wildcard operand dropped the rest in silence.
    
    One correction the re-keying forced into the open: a selector reading a
    position whose value came from a materialized default read the AUTHORED
    graph, which does not hold it. It now reads what the walk validated.
    
    PARITY: the intended deltas are exactly the two ratified ones — findings
    appearing where crosstalk wrongly suppressed them, and the refusal going
    loud. No other suite changed.
    
    Gates: typecheck 0, lint 0, adr-check OK, definition 530/530; full serial
    suite 2024 passed with the one pre-existing J-RESPAWN flake, which
    reproduces on the pre-block commit and passes 4/4 alone.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

+/**
+ * A delegation the registry REFUSED. The engine cannot validate the config
+ * it was handed, so the obligation the declaration placed on that instance
+ * is UNDISCHARGED — and an undischarged obligation that says nothing is
+ * the same defect as a rule that quietly does not run: the config
+ * disappeared from the admitted value and no finding recorded it.
+ *
+ * It travels to the end of the walk rather than reporting on the spot,
+ * because a sibling lane may yet fail and become the trace (the measured
+ * shape: a binding whose `uses` is itself checked against the same
+ * registry). What is owed is per INSTANCE — one binding's unresolvable
+ * registration says nothing about another's.
+ */
+interface Obligation {
+  readonly frame: Frame;
+  readonly by: string;
+  readonly dependsOn: readonly string[];
+}

+/**
+ * The INSTANCE address of a position — the walk's own segment list.
+ *
+ * It is not `frame.path`: a finding path can be COARSENED by a declared
+ * grain (`memberLaneAt: "container"` reports every member at the list's
+ * own path), and two positions that report at one path are still two
+ * positions. Nor is it `frame.at.decl`: that names the DECLARATION, which
+ * every instance of it shares — which is how one open-map entry's broken
+ * field came to decide another entry's rule.
+ */
+function instanceKey(segments: readonly (string | number)[]): string {
+  return JSON.stringify(segments);
+}
+
+/** `tag@scope`, for a status a sibling asks about by name. */
+function tagKey(scope: readonly (string | number)[], tag: string): string {
+  return `${tag}\u0000${instanceKey(scope)}`;
+}

+  /**
+   * Record a tag's status twice: at the node's OWN instance, so a rule
+   * nested inside it finds exactly that node's answer, and at its
+   * CONTAINER's, so a sibling finds it. Two entries of one open map write
+   * different own-keys, which is what stops one entry answering for
+   * another.
+   */
+  markTag(frame: Frame, tag: string, ok: boolean): void {
+    for (const scope of [frame.segments, frame.parentSegments ?? []]) {
+      const key = tagKey(scope, tag);
+      if (this.tagOk.get(key) !== false) this.tagOk.set(key, ok);
+    }
+    if (!ok) this.tagFailedAnywhere.add(tag);
+  }
+
+  /** The status of `tag` as the instance at `scope` means it: the nearest
+   * answer walking outward from itself. Silence is not failure — an
+   * unrecorded tag suppresses nothing. */
+  reliableTag(scope: readonly (string | number)[], tag: string): boolean {
+    for (let cut = scope.length; cut >= 0; cut -= 1) {
+      const recorded = this.tagOk.get(tagKey(scope.slice(0, cut), tag));
+      if (recorded !== undefined) return recorded;
+    }
+    return true;
+  }
+

   519	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   520	// The run state.
   521	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   522	
   523	interface Frame {
   524	  /** The declaration position being evaluated: its node and the address the
   525	   * walk records for it (`$.steps.*.role`). One value, so the address and
   526	   * the node it names cannot come apart. */
   527	  readonly at: DeclCursor;
   528	  /** The position of the enclosing container — the declaration operand of a
   529	   * `^` selector, exactly as `parentValue` is its value operand. It is NOT
   530	   * derived by chopping `at.decl`: a list member's address ends in `[]`,
   531	   * which chopping resolves to the wrong container. */
   532	  readonly parentAt?: DeclCursor | undefined;
   533	  /** The enclosing container's INSTANCE address, travelling beside
   534	   * `parentValue` and `parentAt` so all three describe the same
   535	   * container. */
   536	  readonly parentSegments?: readonly (string | number)[] | undefined;
   537	  /** The value path the findings address (`steps.review.role`). */
   538	  readonly path: string;
   539	  /** The SOURCE-document address, carried segment by segment as the walk
   540	   * descends. It is not derived from `path`: a rendered path is lossy —
   541	   * an authored id may itself contain `[0]` or a dot, and re-parsing one
   542	   * addresses a different node or none (B1 F3). */
   543	  readonly segments: readonly (string | number)[];
   544	  readonly value: unknown;
   545	  /** The enclosing container's value — the operand of a `..` selector. */
   546	  readonly parentValue: unknown;
   547	  /** The key of the nearest enclosing OPEN-map entry. */
   548	  readonly ownerKey?: string | undefined;
   549	  readonly label?: string | undefined;
   550	}
   551	
   552	/** What a node evaluation reports upward: whether the node produced a
   553	 * value its dependents may rely on (the implicit container precondition
   554	 * plus every DECLARED `gating`). */
   555	interface NodeResult {
   556	  readonly ok: boolean;
   557	  readonly value?: unknown;
   558	}
   559	
   560	/**
   561	 * A delegation the registry REFUSED. The engine cannot validate the config
   562	 * it was handed, so the obligation the declaration placed on that instance
   563	 * is UNDISCHARGED — and an undischarged obligation that says nothing is
   564	 * the same defect as a rule that quietly does not run: the config
   565	 * disappeared from the admitted value and no finding recorded it.
   566	 *
   567	 * It travels to the end of the walk rather than reporting on the spot,
   568	 * because a sibling lane may yet fail and become the trace (the measured
   569	 * shape: a binding whose `uses` is itself checked against the same
   570	 * registry). What is owed is per INSTANCE — one binding's unresolvable
   571	 * registration says nothing about another's.
   572	 */
   573	interface Obligation {
   574	  readonly frame: Frame;
   575	  readonly by: string;
   576	  readonly dependsOn: readonly string[];
   577	}
   578	
   579	/** A membership lane whose operand has NOT been evaluated yet. The engine
   580	 * defers it to the end of the walk rather than reading an operand whose
   581	 * reliability is still unknown — a GENERAL property of the `memberOf`
   582	 * construct, not a per-rule placement. */
   583	interface DeferredCheck {
   584	  readonly frame: Frame;
   585	  readonly rule: MembershipRule;
   586	  readonly candidate: unknown;
   587	  readonly slots: Slots;
   588	}
   589	
   590	/**
   591	 * The INSTANCE address of a position — the walk's own segment list.
   592	 *
   593	 * It is not `frame.path`: a finding path can be COARSENED by a declared
   594	 * grain (`memberLaneAt: "container"` reports every member at the list's
   595	 * own path), and two positions that report at one path are still two
   596	 * positions. Nor is it `frame.at.decl`: that names the DECLARATION, which
   597	 * every instance of it shares — which is how one open-map entry's broken
   598	 * field came to decide another entry's rule.
   599	 */
   600	function instanceKey(segments: readonly (string | number)[]): string {
   601	  return JSON.stringify(segments);
   602	}
   603	
   604	/** `tag@scope`, for a status a sibling asks about by name. */
   605	function tagKey(scope: readonly (string | number)[], tag: string): string {
   606	  return `${tag}\u0000${instanceKey(scope)}`;
   607	}
   608	
   609	class Run {
   610	  readonly findings: ValidationFinding[] = [];
   611	  readonly effectiveConfigs = new Map<string, unknown>();
   612	  readonly runtimeContextBindings: string[] = [];
   613	  /** INSTANCE address → whether THAT instance produced a usable value. A
   614	   * rule naming an unreliable operand is suppressed; keyed per instance,
   615	   * a sibling's failure cannot suppress a rule that never read it. */
   616	  readonly declOk = new Map<string, boolean>();
   617	  /** Instance addresses whose evaluation has completed. An operand that is
   618	   * neither reliable nor unreliable but PENDING defers its rule. */
   619	  readonly completed = new Set<string>();
   620	  /** `dependsOn` names a TAG, and what the citing instance means by it is
   621	   * "that tag AT MY SCOPE" — its own container's, or the nearest enclosing
   622	   * one. Recorded per scope so the answer is the citing instance's, and
   623	   * looked up outward so a member can still depend on an ancestor's
   624	   * sibling. */
   625	  readonly tagOk = new Map<string, boolean>();
   626	  /** Every tag that failed ANYWHERE. A cross rule is document-scoped, so
   627	   * this is the status it means. */
   628	  readonly tagFailedAnywhere = new Set<string>();
   629	  /** Instance address → the value a declared `default:` materialized
   630	   * there. The authored graph does not hold it, and a selector must read
   631	   * what the walk validated rather than the absence it replaced. */
   632	  readonly materialized = new Map<string, unknown>();
   633	  readonly deferred: DeferredCheck[] = [];
   634	  /** Delegations whose registration the registry refused. What is still
   635	   * OWED travels per instance to the end of the walk: a config may not
   636	   * vanish from the admitted value with nothing recording that it was
   637	   * never validated. */
   638	  readonly obligations: Obligation[] = [];
   639	  readonly memo = new WeakMap<object, unknown>();
   640	  root: unknown;
   641	
   642	  constructor(
   643	    readonly surface: SurfaceDecl,
   644	    readonly channel: EngineChannel,
   645	    readonly registries: Readonly<Record<string, GateCatalog | undefined>>,
   646	  ) {}
   647	
   648	  get valueClasses(): Readonly<Record<string, NodeDecl>> {
   649	    return this.surface.valueClasses;
   650	  }
   651	
   652	  /** The document root as a declaration position — the operand of every
   653	   * `$`-rooted selector. */
   654	  get rootAt(): DeclCursor {
   655	    return { node: this.surface.root, decl: "$" };
   656	  }
   657	
   658	  /** The injected set a selector root or a delegation names. */
   659	  registry(name: string): GateCatalog | undefined {
   660	    return this.registries[name];
   661	  }
   662	
   663	  emit(path: string, message: string, code?: string): void {
   664	    this.findings.push(code === undefined ? { path, message } : { path, message, code });
   665	  }
   666	
   667	  mark(key: string, ok: boolean): void {
   668	    if (this.declOk.get(key) === false) return;
   669	    this.declOk.set(key, ok);
   670	  }
   671	
   672	  /**
   673	   * Record a tag's status twice: at the node's OWN instance, so a rule
   674	   * nested inside it finds exactly that node's answer, and at its
   675	   * CONTAINER's, so a sibling finds it. Two entries of one open map write
   676	   * different own-keys, which is what stops one entry answering for
   677	   * another.
   678	   */
   679	  markTag(frame: Frame, tag: string, ok: boolean): void {
   680	    for (const scope of [frame.segments, frame.parentSegments ?? []]) {
   681	      const key = tagKey(scope, tag);
   682	      if (this.tagOk.get(key) !== false) this.tagOk.set(key, ok);
   683	    }
   684	    if (!ok) this.tagFailedAnywhere.add(tag);
   685	  }
   686	
   687	  /** The status of `tag` as the instance at `scope` means it: the nearest
   688	   * answer walking outward from itself. Silence is not failure — an
   689	   * unrecorded tag suppresses nothing. */
   690	  reliableTag(scope: readonly (string | number)[], tag: string): boolean {
   691	    for (let cut = scope.length; cut >= 0; cut -= 1) {
   692	      const recorded = this.tagOk.get(tagKey(scope.slice(0, cut), tag));
   693	      if (recorded !== undefined) return recorded;
   694	    }
   695	    return true;
   696	  }
   697	
   698	  reliable(key: string): boolean {
   699	    return this.declOk.get(key) !== false;
   700	  }
   701	}
   702	
   703	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   704	// Selectors.
   705	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   706	
   707	type SelectorResult =
   708	  | { readonly status: "ok"; readonly values: readonly string[] }
   709	  | { readonly status: "unreliable" | "pending" };
   710	
   711	const UNRELIABLE = { status: "unreliable" } as const;
   712	const PENDING = { status: "pending" } as const;
   713	
   714	/** Walk a selector path over the value graph, expanding `*` over open maps.
   715	 * `^` resolves to the citing node's own container. A prefix that has not
   716	 * been evaluated yet answers PENDING, not "unreliable".
   717	 *
   718	 * The DECLARATION side of this walk goes through `descend` — the same call
   719	 * the load-time gate makes — so a path the gate resolved is a path this
   720	 * walk reaches, by construction rather than by two implementations
   721	 * agreeing. */
   722	function resolvePath(
   723	  run: Run,
   724	  from: Frame,
   725	  path: DeclPath,
   726	): { readonly status: "ok"; readonly nodes: unknown[] } | { readonly status: "unreliable" | "pending" } {
   727	  const segments = path.split(".");
   728	  // Each node carries its OWN instance address, because reliability and
   729	  // completion are that instance's, not its declaration's.
   730	  let nodes: { readonly value: unknown; readonly segments: readonly (string | number)[] }[];
   731	  let cursor: DeclCursor | undefined;
   732	  const head = segments.shift();
   733	  if (head === "$") {
   734	    nodes = [{ value: run.root, segments: [] }];
   735	    cursor = run.rootAt;
   736	  } else if (head === "^") {
   737	    // Parent-relative: the citing node's own container.
   738	    nodes = [{ value: from.parentValue, segments: from.parentSegments ?? [] }];
   739	    cursor = from.parentAt;
   740	  } else {
   741	    return UNRELIABLE;
   742	  }
   743	  if (cursor === undefined) return UNRELIABLE;
   744	  for (const segment of segments) {
   745	    // Nothing left to walk: an open map with no entries ends the walk
   746	    // here, and the honest answer is the EMPTY set, decided now. Asking
   747	    // whether the positions below it have been evaluated would wait on
   748	    // instances that do not exist — a rule over an empty map would sit
   749	    // undecided forever for want of an operand it can already see is
   750	    // empty.
   751	    if (nodes.length === 0) return { status: "ok", nodes: [] };
   752	    const next = descend(run.surface, cursor, selectorStep(segment));
   753	    if (next === undefined) return UNRELIABLE;
   754	    cursor = next;
   755	    const values: { readonly value: unknown; readonly segments: readonly (string | number)[] }[] = [];
   756	    for (const node of nodes) {
   757	      if (!isContainer(node.value)) return UNRELIABLE;
   758	      const container = node.value;
   759	      const keys =
   760	        segment === "*"
   761	          ? entriesOf(container).map(([key]) => key).filter((key): key is string => typeof key === "string")
   762	          : [segment];
   763	      for (const key of keys) {
   764	        const childSegments = [...node.segments, key];
   765	        const instance = instanceKey(childSegments);
   766	        // Asked of the REACHED INSTANCE, and asked BEFORE the value is
   767	        // read: a sibling instance that failed says nothing about this
   768	        // one, and a key that is simply absent has no instance to be
   769	        // reliable OR unreliable — it is UNDECIDED, which is the answer
   770	        // that gets reported rather than dropped.
   771	        if (!run.reliable(instance)) return UNRELIABLE;
   772	        if (!run.completed.has(instance)) return PENDING;
   773	        values.push({
   774	          // A materialized default lives at its instance, not in the
   775	          // authored graph — the walk validated it, so a selector reads
   776	          // the same value the walk did.
   777	          value: containerHas(container, key) ? containerGet(container, key) : run.materialized.get(instance),
   778	          segments: childSegments,
   779	        });
   780	      }
   781	    }
   782	    nodes = values;
   783	  }
   784	  return { status: "ok", nodes: nodes.map((node) => node.value) };
   785	}
   786	
   787	function evaluateSelector(run: Run, from: Frame, selector: Selector): SelectorResult {
   788	  if ("injected" in selector) {
   789	    // D8's ADMITTED widening: the selector root is an INJECTED set. A
   790	    // registry resolves rather than enumerates, so membership is asked of
   791	    // it directly (`isMember`); the set itself is never listed.
   792	    return run.registry(selector.injected) === undefined ? UNRELIABLE : { status: "ok", values: [] };
   793	  }
   794	  if ("union" in selector) {
   795	    const parts = selector.union.map((part) => evaluateSelector(run, from, part));
   796	    if (parts.some((part) => part.status === "pending")) return PENDING;
   797	    if (parts.some((part) => part.status === "unreliable")) return UNRELIABLE;
   798	    return {
   799	      status: "ok",
   800	      values: parts.flatMap((part) => (part.status === "ok" ? part.values : [])),
   801	    };
   802	  }
   803	  const read = selectorRead(selector);
   804	  const resolved = resolvePath(run, from, read.path);
   805	  if (resolved.status !== "ok") return resolved;
   806	  const values: string[] = [];
   807	  for (const node of resolved.nodes) {
   808	    // The SAME predicate the gate demands every legal value satisfy.
   809	    if (!RELATION_READS[read.relation].test(node)) return UNRELIABLE;
   810	    if (read.relation === "keysOf") {
   811	      for (const [key] of entriesOf(node as AnyMap)) if (typeof key === "string") values.push(key);
   812	    } else if (read.relation === "valuesOf") {
   813	      for (const item of node as readonly unknown[]) if (typeof item === "string") values.push(item);
   814	    } else {
   815	      values.push(node as string);
   816	    }
   817	  }
   818	  return { status: "ok", values };
   819	}
   820	
   821	/** `memberOf` against an INJECTED root asks the registry; against a
   822	 * document root it asks the collected set. */
   823	function isMember(run: Run, selector: Selector, set: SelectorResult, candidate: string): boolean {
   824	  if ("injected" in selector) return run.registry(selector.injected)?.resolve(candidate) != null;
   825	  return set.status === "ok" && set.values.includes(candidate);
   826	}
   827	
   828	function checkMembership(
   829	  run: Run,
   830	  frame: Frame,
   831	  rule: MembershipRule,
   832	  candidate: unknown,
   833	  slots: Slots,
   834	): void {
   835	  // Suppression is the CITING INSTANCE's business, and it is now nothing
   836	  // more than that: `reliableTag` answers from the citing instance
   837	  // outward, so the sibling map this used to need — and the ordering bug
   838	  // that made one open-map entry's failure suppress a different, valid
   839	  // entry's finding — are both gone with the coarse key that caused them.
   840	  const suppressed = rule.dependsOn?.some((tag) => !run.reliableTag(frame.segments, tag));
   841	  if (suppressed === true) return;
   842	  const set = evaluateSelector(run, frame, rule.target);
   843	  if (set.status === "pending") {
   844	    // The decision is taken NOW, while the siblings are in scope: by the
   845	    // time the queue drains they are gone.
   846	    run.deferred.push({ frame, rule, candidate, slots });
   847	    return;
   848	  }
   849	  if (set.status === "unreliable") return;
   850	  const at = rule.at === "container" ? parentPath(frame.path) : frame.path;
   851	  const hit = typeof candidate === "string" && isMember(run, rule.target, set, candidate);
   852	  const violated = rule.relation === "disjointFrom" ? hit : !hit;
   853	  if (violated) {
   854	    run.emit(at, render(rule.message, { ...slots, path: at, value: candidate }), rule.code);
   855	  }
   856	}
   857	
   858	function parentPath(path: string): string {
   859	  const bracket = path.lastIndexOf("[");
   860	  const dot = path.lastIndexOf(".");
   861	  if (bracket > dot) return path.slice(0, bracket);
   862	  if (dot < 0) return "$";
   863	  return path.slice(0, dot);
   864	}
   865	
   866	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   867	// The source-form ladder (vocabulary #6) — file channel only.
   868	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   869	
   870	const PLAIN_DECIMAL = /^[1-9][0-9]*$/;
   871	
   872	/**
   873	 * The `plainDecimalInteger` ladder over a YAML node: alias-free,
   874	 * scalar, anchor-free, tag-free, and a `^[1-9][0-9]*$` raw slice, then
   875	 * the resolved safe-integer belt. Its six messages are FIXED by the
   876	 * ladder (they are identical at every measured user), so they live with
   877	 * the construct rather than being restated per node.
   878	 */
   879	const SOURCE_LADDER = {
   880	  alias: "{path} must not be an alias (an alias hides the source form, probe P21)",
   881	  nonScalar: "{path} must be a plain decimal integer scalar",
   882	  anchor: "{path} must not carry an anchor (the anchor token sits outside the range slice)",
   883	  tag: "{path} must not carry a tag (probe P22: a tag can flip the resolved type silently)",
   884	  form: "{path} must be written as a plain decimal integer >= 1; got source form {source}",
   885	  resolved: "{path} must resolve to a safe integer >= 1",
   886	} as const;
   887	
   888	export function sourceLadderFinding(node: unknown, source: string, path: string): string | undefined {
   889	  if (node === undefined || node === null) return undefined;
   890	  if (isAlias(node)) return render(SOURCE_LADDER.alias, { path });
   891	  if (!isScalar(node)) return render(SOURCE_LADDER.nonScalar, { path });
   892	  if (node.anchor !== undefined) return render(SOURCE_LADDER.anchor, { path });
   893	  if (node.tag !== undefined) return render(SOURCE_LADDER.tag, { path });
   894	  const range = node.range;
   895	  const slice = range ? source.slice(range[0], range[1]) : "";
   896	  if (!PLAIN_DECIMAL.test(slice)) {
   897	    return render(SOURCE_LADDER.form, { path, source: JSON.stringify(slice) });
   898	  }
   899	  const resolved: unknown = node.value;
   900	  if (typeof resolved !== "number" || !Number.isSafeInteger(resolved) || resolved < 1) {

   906	
   907	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   908	// The acyclic-graph lane (the substrate's `resolve.graph`).
   909	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   910	
   911	/** DFS with a PATH-SCOPED ancestor set: shared subtrees (legal anchor
   912	 * reuse) are not cycles; only a back-edge is. */
   913	function findCycle(value: unknown, path: string, ancestors: Set<object>): string | undefined {
   914	  if (typeof value !== "object" || value === null) return undefined;
   915	  if (ancestors.has(value)) return path;
   916	  ancestors.add(value);
   917	  try {
   918	    if (Array.isArray(value)) {
   919	      for (let i = 0; i < value.length; i += 1) {
   920	        const hit = findCycle(value[i], joinPath(path, String(i)), ancestors);
   921	        if (hit !== undefined) return hit;
   922	      }
   923	      return undefined;
   924	    }
   925	    if (isResolvedMap(value)) {
   926	      for (const [key, child] of value.entries()) {
   927	        const segment = typeof key === "string" ? key : "<map-key>";
   928	        const keyHit = findCycle(key, joinPath(path, `${segment}<key>`), ancestors);
   929	        if (keyHit !== undefined) return keyHit;
   930	        const valueHit = findCycle(child, joinPath(path, segment), ancestors);
   931	        if (valueHit !== undefined) return valueHit;
   932	      }
   933	    }
   934	    return undefined;
   935	  } finally {
   936	    ancestors.delete(value);
   937	  }
   938	}
   939	
   940	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   941	// Node evaluation.
   942	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   943	
   944	const DEFAULT_MISSING = 'missing required key "{key}"';
   945	
   946	/** Sibling reliability inside ONE fixed-map evaluation. `dependsOn` reads
   947	 * it before the global map so a defect in binding #1 cannot suppress a
   948	 * lane in binding #2. */
   949	function baseSlots(frame: Frame): Slots {
   950	  return { path: frame.path, value: frame.value, ownerKey: frame.ownerKey, label: frame.label };
   951	}
   952	
   953	function evaluateNode(run: Run, decl: NodeDecl, frame: Frame): NodeResult {
   954	  const result = dispatch(run, decl, frame);
   955	  const instance = instanceKey(frame.segments);
   956	  run.completed.add(instance);
   957	  run.mark(instance, result.ok);
   958	  run.markTag(frame, decl.tag, result.ok);
   959	  return result;
   960	}
   961	
   962	function dispatch(run: Run, decl: NodeDecl, frame: Frame): NodeResult {
   963	  switch (decl.kind) {
   964	    case "map.fixed":
   965	      return evalMapFixed(run, decl, frame);
   966	    case "map.open":
   967	      return evalMapOpen(run, decl, frame);
   968	    case "map.plain":
   969	      return evalMapPlain(run, decl, frame);
   970	    case "list":
   971	      return evalList(run, decl, frame);
   972	    case "string":
   973	      return evalString(run, decl, frame);
   974	    case "integer":
   975	      return evalInteger(run, decl, frame);
   976	    case "enum":
   977	      return evalEnum(run, decl, frame);
   978	    case "union":
   979	      return evalUnion(run, decl, frame);
   980	    case "raw":
   981	      return evalRaw(run, decl, frame);
   982	    case "valueClass":
   983	      return evalValueClassRef(run, decl, frame);
   984	    case "delegate":
   985	      return evalDelegate(run, decl, frame);
   986	  }
   987	}
   988	
   989	function evalValueClassRef(
   990	  run: Run,
   991	  decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "valueClass" }>,
   992	  frame: Frame,
   993	): NodeResult {
   994	  const target = run.valueClasses[decl.valueClass];
   995	  if (target === undefined) return { ok: true, value: frame.value };
   996	  const merged: Frame = { ...frame, label: decl.label ?? frame.label };
   997	  const result = dispatch(run, target, merged);
   998	  // The REFERENCING site's gating decides, not the shared class's.
   999	  if (!result.ok && decl.gating === true) run.mark(instanceKey(frame.segments), false);
  1000	  return result;
  1001	}
  1002	
  1003	function evalMapFixed(
  1004	  run: Run,
  1005	  decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "map.fixed" }>,
  1006	  frame: Frame,
  1007	): NodeResult {
  1008	  const value = frame.value;
  1009	  const slots = baseSlots(frame);
  1010	  if (!isContainer(value)) {
  1011	    run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.containerMessage, slots));
  1012	    return { ok: false };
  1013	  }
  1014	  const container = value;
  1015	  // Every field position resolved ONCE, through the engine's own descent —
  1016	  // the same call the gate makes to resolve a reference TO one of them.
  1017	  const fields = new Map<string, DeclCursor>();
  1018	  for (const name of Object.keys(decl.fields)) {
  1019	    const at = descend(run.surface, frame.at, { kind: "field", name });
  1020	    if (at !== undefined) fields.set(name, at);
  1021	  }
  1022	  const fieldNames = [...fields.keys()];
  1023	  // A field declared for ONE channel is legal only there — the same
  1024	  // channel scoping D1 applies to source-bearing attributes, applied to
  1025	  // keyset membership.
  1026	  const inChannel = (name: string): boolean => {
  1027	    const channel = fields.get(name)?.node.channel;
  1028	    return channel === undefined || channel === "both" || channel === run.channel.kind;
  1029	  };
  1030	  // A channel-scoped field is scoped WHOLE: out of channel it is neither a
  1031	  // legal key, nor a missing one, nor evaluated (design review F7).
  1032	  const scoped = fieldNames.filter(inChannel);
  1033	  const legal = new Set(scoped);
  1034	  const normalized: Record<string, unknown> = {};
  1035	
  1036	  const emitUnknownKeys = (): void => {
  1037	    for (const [key] of entriesOf(container)) {
  1038	      if (typeof key !== "string") {
  1039	        run.emit(
  1040	          frame.path,
  1041	          render(decl.unknownMessage, { ...slots, key, value: key, keys: scoped.join(", ") }),
  1042	        );
  1043	        continue;
  1044	      }
  1045	      if (legal.has(key)) continue;
  1046	      const at = joinPath(frame.path, key);
  1047	      const removed = ownMessage(decl.removedKeys, key);
  1048	      const keySlots: Slots = { ...slots, path: at, key, value: key, keys: scoped.join(", ") };
  1049	      run.emit(at, render(removed ?? decl.unknownMessage, keySlots));
  1050	    }
  1051	  };
  1052	
  1053	  const emitMissing = (name: string): void => {
  1054	    const fieldAt = fields.get(name);
  1055	    const field = fieldAt?.node;
  1056	    const presence = field?.presence;
  1057	    if (fieldAt === undefined || field === undefined || presence?.required !== true) return;
  1058	    if (containerHas(container, name)) return;
  1059	    if (presence.foldedIntoTypeLane === true) {
  1060	      // Absence is this node's OWN type lane (a binding's `uses`).
  1061	      const folded = childFrame(frame, fieldAt, name, undefined, container);
  1062	      evaluateNode(run, field, folded);
  1063	      run.markTag(folded, field.tag, false);
  1064	      return;
  1065	    }
  1066	    const at = presence.at === "self" ? joinPath(frame.path, name) : frame.path;
  1067	    run.emit(
  1068	      at,
  1069	      render(presence.message ?? decl.missingMessage ?? DEFAULT_MISSING, { ...slots, path: at, key: name }),
  1070	      presence.code,
  1071	    );
  1072	    // The position is UNRELIABLE, not merely unvisited: a rule reading it
  1073	    // is suppressed, and the missing-key finding is the trace that
  1074	    // explains why. Without this the lane would instead end the walk
  1075	    // UNDECIDED and report a second time for a fault already named.
  1076	    const missing = childFrame(frame, fieldAt, name, undefined, container);
  1077	    run.mark(instanceKey(missing.segments), false);
  1078	    run.markTag(missing, field.tag, false);
  1079	  };
  1080	
  1081	  const evalOneField = (name: string): void => {
  1082	    const at = fields.get(name);
  1083	    if (at === undefined) return;
  1084	    const field = at.node;
  1085	    const present = containerHas(container, name);
  1086	    // An ABSENT field is where a declared `default:` materializes — the
  1087	    // schema-side half of ADR-019 D3. It takes the SAME path an authored
  1088	    // value takes, because materializing a value is not an exemption from
  1089	    // validating it: what reaches the admitted form is the validated
  1090	    // result (an enum's STORED token, not its authored spelling), and the
  1091	    // position COMPLETES, so a rule watching it is decided against the
  1092	    // default rather than left undecided by an absence the declaration
  1093	    // itself filled.
  1094	    if (!present && field.default === undefined) return;
  1095	    const child = childFrame(frame, at, name, present ? containerGet(container, name) : field.default, container);
  1096	    const result = evaluateNode(run, field, child);
  1097	    // A materialized default is not in the authored graph, so a selector
  1098	    // reading this position must be able to find what the walk validated.
  1099	    if (!present) run.materialized.set(instanceKey(child.segments), result.value ?? field.default);
  1100	    if (result.ok && result.value !== undefined) defineOwn(normalized, name, result.value);
  1101	  };
  1102	
  1103	  const order = decl.laneOrder ?? "unknownThenPerKey";
  1104	  if (order === "missingThenUnknown") {
  1105	    for (const name of scoped) emitMissing(name);
  1106	    emitUnknownKeys();
  1107	    for (const name of scoped) evalOneField(name);
  1108	  } else if (order === "unknownThenMissingThenValues") {
  1109	    emitUnknownKeys();
  1110	    for (const name of scoped) emitMissing(name);
  1111	    for (const name of scoped) evalOneField(name);
  1112	  } else {
  1113	    emitUnknownKeys();
  1114	    for (const name of scoped) {
  1115	      if (!containerHas(container, name)) {
  1116	        emitMissing(name);
  1117	        evalOneField(name);
  1118	        continue;
  1119	      }
  1120	      evalOneField(name);
  1121	    }
  1122	  }
  1123	  return { ok: true, value: normalized };
  1124	}
  1125	
  1126	function childFrame(parent: Frame, at: DeclCursor, key: string, value: unknown, parentValue: unknown): Frame {
  1127	  return {
  1128	    at,
  1129	    parentAt: parent.at,
  1130	    parentSegments: parent.segments,
  1131	    path: joinPath(parent.path, key),
  1132	    segments: [...parent.segments, key],
  1133	    value,
  1134	    parentValue,
  1135	    ownerKey: parent.ownerKey,
  1136	  };
  1137	}
  1138	
  1139	function evalMapOpen(
  1140	  run: Run,
  1141	  decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "map.open" }>,
  1142	  frame: Frame,
  1143	): NodeResult {
  1144	  const value = frame.value;
  1145	  const slots = baseSlots(frame);
  1146	  if (!isContainer(value)) {
  1147	    run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.containerMessage, slots));
  1148	    return { ok: false };
  1149	  }
  1150	  const container = value;
  1151	  const entries = entriesOf(container);
  1152	  let ok = true;
  1153	  if (decl.nonempty !== undefined && entries.length === 0) {
  1154	    run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.nonempty.message, slots));
  1155	    if (decl.nonempty.gating === true) ok = false;
  1156	  }
  1157	  if (decl.deepKeyStringness?.channel === run.channel.kind) {
  1158	    scanKeyStringness(run, container, frame.path, decl.deepKeyStringness.message, new WeakSet());
  1159	  }
  1160	  const keyAt = descend(run.surface, frame.at, { kind: "keyClass" });
  1161	  const entryAt = descend(run.surface, frame.at, { kind: "entry" });
  1162	  const normalized: Record<string, unknown> = {};
  1163	  for (const [key, child] of entries) {
  1164	    const keyIsString = typeof key === "string";
  1165	    const entryPath = keyIsString ? joinPath(frame.path, key) : frame.path;
  1166	    if (decl.keyClass !== undefined && keyAt !== undefined) {
  1167	      const keyFrame: Frame = {
  1168	        at: keyAt,
  1169	        parentAt: frame.at,
  1170	        parentSegments: frame.segments,
  1171	        path: decl.keyLaneAt === "container" ? frame.path : entryPath,
  1172	        segments: frame.segments,
  1173	        value: key,
  1174	        parentValue: container,
  1175	        ownerKey: frame.ownerKey,
  1176	      };
  1177	      const keyResult = evaluateNode(run, decl.keyClass, keyFrame);
  1178	      if (!keyResult.ok && decl.keyClass.gating === true) ok = false;
  1179	    }
  1180	    if (decl.keysSubsetOf !== undefined) {
  1181	      const before = run.findings.length;
  1182	      // The rule is the OPEN MAP's, so a `^`-relative selector resolves
  1183	      // from the map's own container — never from the map itself.
  1184	      const subsetFrame: Frame = {
  1185	        at: frame.at,
  1186	        parentAt: frame.parentAt,
  1187	        parentSegments: frame.parentSegments,
  1188	        path: entryPath,
  1189	        segments: frame.segments,
  1190	        value: key,
  1191	        parentValue: frame.parentValue,
  1192	        ownerKey: frame.ownerKey,
  1193	      };
  1194	      checkMembership(run, subsetFrame, decl.keysSubsetOf, key, {
  1195	        ...slots,
  1196	        path: entryPath,
  1197	        key,
  1198	        ownerKey: frame.ownerKey,
  1199	      });
  1200	      // A key outside the subset is DEAD config: nothing below it has an
  1201	      // operand to be validated against (the measured lane's CONTINUE).
  1202	      if (run.findings.length > before) continue;
  1203	    }
  1204	    if (entryAt === undefined) continue;
  1205	    const entryFrame: Frame = {
  1206	      at: entryAt,
  1207	      parentAt: frame.at,
  1208	      parentSegments: frame.segments,
  1209	      path: entryPath,
  1210	      segments: keyIsString ? [...frame.segments, key] : frame.segments,
  1211	      value: child,
  1212	      parentValue: container,
  1213	      ownerKey: keyIsString ? key : frame.ownerKey,
  1214	    };
  1215	    const entryResult = evaluateNode(run, decl.entry, entryFrame);
  1216	    if (keyIsString && entryResult.ok && entryResult.value !== undefined) {
  1217	      defineOwn(normalized, key, entryResult.value);
  1218	    }
  1219	  }
  1220	  return { ok, value: normalized };
  1221	}
  1222	
  1223	/** The gates subtree's file-channel key-STRINGNESS scan: a non-string key
  1224	 * cannot become an own property and would blind every own-key scan, so it
  1225	 * reports at the NEAREST addressable path (the containing map's). */
  1226	function scanKeyStringness(
  1227	  run: Run,
  1228	  value: unknown,
  1229	  path: string,
  1230	  message: MessageTemplate,
  1231	  seen: WeakSet<object>,
  1232	): void {
  1233	  if (typeof value !== "object" || value === null) return;
  1234	  if (seen.has(value)) return;
  1235	  seen.add(value);
  1236	  if (Array.isArray(value)) {
  1237	    value.forEach((item, index) => scanKeyStringness(run, item, indexPath(path, index), message, seen));
  1238	    return;
  1239	  }
  1240	  if (!isResolvedMap(value)) return;
  1241	  for (const [key, child] of value.entries()) {
  1242	    if (typeof key !== "string") {
  1243	      run.emit(path, render(message, { path, key, value: key }));
  1244	      continue;
  1245	    }
  1246	    scanKeyStringness(run, child, joinPath(path, key), message, seen);
  1247	  }
  1248	}
  1249	
  1250	function evalList(
  1251	  run: Run,
  1252	  decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "list" }>,
  1253	  frame: Frame,
  1254	): NodeResult {
  1255	  const value = frame.value;
  1256	  const slots = baseSlots(frame);
  1257	  if (!Array.isArray(value)) {
  1258	    run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.containerMessage, slots));
  1259	    return { ok: false };
  1260	  }
  1261	  let ok = true;
  1262	  if (decl.nonempty !== undefined && value.length === 0) {
  1263	    run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.nonempty.message, slots));
  1264	    if (decl.nonempty.gating === true) ok = false;
  1265	  }
  1266	  const memberAt = descend(run.surface, frame.at, { kind: "member" });
  1267	  if (memberAt === undefined) return { ok, value: [] };
  1268	  const normalized: unknown[] = [];
  1269	  const seen = new Set<unknown>();
  1270	  const clean: { readonly member: unknown; readonly path: string }[] = [];
  1271	  value.forEach((member, index) => {
  1272	    const memberPath = decl.memberLaneAt === "container" ? frame.path : indexPath(frame.path, index);
  1273	    const memberFrame: Frame = {
  1274	      at: memberAt,
  1275	      // A list member's `^` operand is the LIST, matching `parentValue`.
  1276	      parentAt: frame.at,
  1277	      parentSegments: frame.segments,
  1278	      path: memberPath,
  1279	      segments: [...frame.segments, index],
  1280	      value: member,
  1281	      parentValue: value,
  1282	      ownerKey: frame.ownerKey,
  1283	    };
  1284	    const before = run.findings.length;
  1285	    const result = evaluateNode(run, decl.member, memberFrame);
  1286	    if (run.findings.length > before) return;
  1287	    if (result.value !== undefined) normalized.push(result.value);
  1288	    if (decl.memberOf !== undefined) {
  1289	      checkMembership(run, memberFrame, decl.memberOf, member, { ...slots, path: memberPath, value: member });
  1290	    }
  1291	    if (decl.unique?.grain === "perOccurrence") {
  1292	      // `unique.at` is the DUPLICATE lane's own grain; it must not inherit
  1293	      // the member lane's, which `memberLaneAt` sets for a different lane.
  1294	      const at = decl.unique.at === "container" ? frame.path : indexPath(frame.path, index);
  1295	      if (seen.has(member)) {
  1296	        run.emit(at, render(decl.unique.message, { ...slots, path: at, value: member }));
  1297	      }
  1298	      seen.add(member);
  1299	    }
  1300	    clean.push({ member, path: memberPath });
  1301	  });
  1302	  if (decl.disjointFrom !== undefined) {
  1303	    for (const entry of clean) {
  1304	      const memberFrame: Frame = {
  1305	        at: memberAt,
  1306	        parentAt: frame.at,
  1307	      parentSegments: frame.segments,
  1308	        path: entry.path,
  1309	        segments: frame.segments,
  1310	        value: entry.member,
  1311	        parentValue: value,
  1312	        ownerKey: frame.ownerKey,
  1313	      };
  1314	      checkMembership(run, memberFrame, decl.disjointFrom, entry.member, {
  1315	        ...slots,
  1316	        path: entry.path,
  1317	        value: entry.member,
  1318	      });
  1319	    }
  1320	  }
  1321	  return { ok, value: normalized };
  1322	}
  1323	
  1324	function evalString(
  1325	  run: Run,
  1326	  decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "string" }>,
  1327	  frame: Frame,
  1328	): NodeResult {
  1329	  const value = frame.value;
  1330	  const slots: Slots = { ...baseSlots(frame), grammar: decl.grammar?.re };
  1331	  if (typeof value !== "string") {
  1332	    // A node that declares NO type lane leaves the non-string case to its
  1333	    // membership lane, which emits ONE finding for both faults (the
  1334	    // measured form of `start` and of a transition target).
  1335	    const typeLane = decl.typeMessage ?? (decl.memberOf === undefined ? decl.grammar?.message : undefined);
  1336	    if (typeLane !== undefined) {
  1337	      run.emit(frame.path, render(typeLane, slots), decl.presence?.code);
  1338	      return { ok: false };
  1339	    }
  1340	  } else {
  1341	    if (decl.nonempty !== undefined && value.length === 0) {
  1342	      run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.nonempty.message, slots), decl.presence?.code);
  1343	      return { ok: false };
  1344	    }
  1345	    if (decl.grammar !== undefined && !new RegExp(decl.grammar.re, "u").test(value)) {
  1346	      run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.grammar.message, slots));
  1347	      return { ok: false };
  1348	    }
  1349	  }
  1350	  if (decl.memberOf !== undefined) {
  1351	    const before = run.findings.length;
  1352	    checkMembership(run, frame, decl.memberOf, value, slots);
  1353	    if (run.findings.length > before) return { ok: false, value };
  1354	  }
  1355	  return typeof value === "string" ? { ok: true, value } : { ok: false };
  1356	}
  1357	
  1358	function evalInteger(
  1359	  run: Run,
  1360	  decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "integer" }>,
  1361	  frame: Frame,
  1362	): NodeResult {
  1363	  const value = frame.value;
  1364	  if (decl.sourceForm === "plainDecimalInteger" && run.channel.kind === "file") {
  1365	    const node: unknown = run.channel.doc.getIn(frame.segments, true);
  1366	    const message = sourceLadderFinding(node, run.channel.source, frame.path);
  1367	    if (message !== undefined) {
  1368	      run.emit(frame.path, message);
  1369	      return { ok: false };
  1370	    }
  1371	    // The ladder proves the SOURCE form; a declared resolved belt is a
  1372	    // separate obligation and still applies (its own bound may be
  1373	    // stricter than the ladder's built-in one).
  1374	  }
  1375	  const belt = decl.resolvedForm;
  1376	  if (belt?.safeInteger === true) {
  1377	    if (typeof value !== "number" || !Number.isSafeInteger(value) || value < belt.min) {
  1378	      run.emit(frame.path, render(belt.message, baseSlots(frame)), decl.presence?.code);
  1379	      return { ok: false };
  1380	    }
  1381	  }
  1382	  return { ok: true, value };
  1383	}
  1384	
  1385	function evalEnum(
  1386	  run: Run,
  1387	  decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "enum" }>,
  1388	  frame: Frame,
  1389	): NodeResult {
  1390	  const value = frame.value;
  1391	  const legal = decl.members.filter(
  1392	    (one) => one.channel === undefined || one.channel === "both" || one.channel === run.channel.kind,
  1393	  );
  1394	  const slots: Slots = {
  1395	    ...baseSlots(frame),
  1396	    members: legal.map((one) => String(one.value)).join(", "),
  1397	  };
  1398	  const member = legal.find((one) => one.value === value);
  1399	  if (member !== undefined) return { ok: true, value: member.store ?? member.value };
  1400	  run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.message, slots), decl.code);
  1401	  return { ok: false };
  1402	}
  1403	
  1404	function evalUnion(
  1405	  run: Run,
  1406	  decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "union" }>,
  1407	  frame: Frame,
  1408	): NodeResult {
  1409	  const value = frame.value;
  1410	  const slots = baseSlots(frame);
  1411	  if (typeof value === "string") {
  1412	    const removed = ownMessage(decl.removedValues, value);
  1413	    if (removed !== undefined) {
  1414	      run.emit(frame.path, render(removed, slots));
  1415	      return { ok: false };
  1416	    }
  1417	    if (decl.literals?.includes(value) === true) return { ok: true, value };
  1418	  }
  1419	  if (isContainer(value) && decl.mapCase !== undefined) {
  1420	    return evaluateNode(run, decl.mapCase, frame);
  1421	  }
  1422	  run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.message, slots));
  1423	  return { ok: false };
  1424	}
  1425	
  1426	function evalRaw(run: Run, decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "raw" }>, frame: Frame): NodeResult {
  1427	  if (decl.containerMessage !== undefined && !isContainer(frame.value)) {
  1428	    run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.containerMessage, baseSlots(frame)));
  1429	    return { ok: false };
  1430	  }
  1431	  return { ok: true, value: materialize(frame.value, run.memo) };
  1432	}
  1433	
  1434	function evalMapPlain(
  1435	  run: Run,
  1436	  decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "map.plain" }>,
  1437	  frame: Frame,
  1438	): NodeResult {
  1439	  const value = frame.value;
  1440	  const slots = baseSlots(frame);
  1441	  const realized = materialize(value, run.memo);
  1442	  if (!isPlainMap(realized)) {
  1443	    run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.containerMessage, { ...slots, value: realized }));
  1444	    return { ok: false };
  1445	  }
  1446	  if (!isCanonicalizable(realized)) {
  1447	    run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.canonicalJsonSafe.message, slots));
  1448	    return { ok: false };
  1449	  }
  1450	  return { ok: true, value: realized };
  1451	}
  1452	
  1453	function evalDelegate(
  1454	  run: Run,
  1455	  decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "delegate" }>,
  1456	  frame: Frame,
  1457	): NodeResult {
  1458	  if (decl.dependsOn?.some((tag) => !run.reliableTag(frame.segments, tag)) === true) return { ok: true };
  1459	  const owner = frame.parentValue;
  1460	  const by = isContainer(owner) ? containerGet(owner, decl.by) : undefined;
  1461	  if (typeof by !== "string") return { ok: true };
  1462	  const registration = run.registry(decl.registry)?.resolve(by) ?? null;
  1463	  if (registration === null) {
  1464	    // Nothing to validate the config AGAINST. The obligation stands and
  1465	    // travels; it is discharged only if a sibling lane fails and becomes
  1466	    // the trace.
  1467	    run.obligations.push({ frame, by, dependsOn: decl.dependsOn ?? [] });
  1468	    return { ok: false };
  1469	  }
  1470	  if (registration.requiresRuntimeContext) run.runtimeContextBindings.push(parentPath(frame.path));
  1471	  const raw = frame.value === undefined ? undefined : materialize(frame.value, run.memo);
  1472	  const result = registration.validateAndNormalizeConfig(raw);
  1473	  if (result.ok) {
  1474	    run.effectiveConfigs.set(parentPath(frame.path), result.effective);
  1475	    return { ok: true, value: result.effective };
  1476	  }
  1477	  if (result.findings.length === 0) {
  1478	    run.emit(frame.path, render(decl.beltMessage, { ...baseSlots(frame), key: by, value: by }));
  1479	    return { ok: false };
  1480	  }
  1481	  for (const finding of result.findings) {
  1482	    const at = finding.path === "" ? frame.path : `${frame.path}.${finding.path}`;
  1483	    run.emit(at, finding.message, finding.code);
  1484	  }
  1485	  return { ok: false };
  1486	}
  1487	
  1488	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1489	// Cross rules and the deferred queue.
  1490	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1491	
  1492	function unique(values: readonly string[]): string[] {
  1493	  return [...new Set(values)];
  1494	}
  1495	
  1496	/** vocabulary #12's `equals` — two-direction set equality, each direction
  1497	 * carrying its own path grain and wording (ch8-C16's measured form). */
  1498	function evaluateEquals(run: Run, frame: Frame, rule: EqualsRuleDecl): void {
  1499	  // A cross rule is DOCUMENT-scoped: it reads the whole document, so the
  1500	  // status it means is "did this tag fail anywhere", not "at my instance"
  1501	  // — a cross rule has no instance.
  1502	  if (rule.dependsOn?.some((tag) => run.tagFailedAnywhere.has(tag)) === true) return;
  1503	  const left = evaluateSelector(run, frame, rule.left);
  1504	  const right = evaluateSelector(run, frame, rule.right);
  1505	  // A cross rule is never queued, so `pending` HERE — after the deferred
  1506	  // queue has drained and every position has been visited — means the same
  1507	  // thing it means there: an operand nothing ever evaluated.
  1508	  if (left.status === "pending" || right.status === "pending") {
  1509	    if (rule.whenOperandAbsent !== "skip") {
  1510	      const undecided = left.status === "pending" ? rule.left : rule.right;
  1511	      reportUndecided(
  1512	        run,
  1513	        run.surface.substrate.internalFailure.path,
  1514	        `the cross rule ${JSON.stringify(rule.tag)} could not be decided — its operand ` +
  1515	          `${describeSelector(undecided)} names a position the walk never evaluated`,
  1516	      );
  1517	    }
  1518	    return;
  1519	  }
  1520	  if (left.status !== "ok" || right.status !== "ok") return;
  1521	  const declared = unique(left.values);
  1522	  const used = unique(right.values);
  1523	  for (const value of used) {
  1524	    if (declared.includes(value)) continue;
  1525	    const at = render(rule.missingFromLeft.at, { value });
  1526	    run.emit(at, render(rule.missingFromLeft.message, { path: at, value }));
  1527	  }
  1528	  for (const value of declared) {
  1529	    if (used.includes(value)) continue;
  1530	    const at = render(rule.missingFromRight.at, { value });
  1531	    run.emit(at, render(rule.missingFromRight.message, { path: at, value }));
  1532	  }
  1533	}
  1534	
  1535	/** How a selector reads, in words a finding can carry. */
  1536	function describeSelector(selector: Selector): string {
  1537	  if ("injected" in selector) return `the injected set ${JSON.stringify(selector.injected)}`;
  1538	  if ("union" in selector) return selector.union.map(describeSelector).join(" + ");
  1539	  const read = selectorRead(selector);
  1540	  return `${read.relation}(${JSON.stringify(read.path)})`;
  1541	}
  1542	
  1543	/**
  1544	 * A lane still UNDECIDED when the walk ends. Its target position was never
  1545	 * evaluated — a field the path runs through is absent — so unlike a
  1546	 * SUPPRESSED lane there is no sibling finding anywhere that explains it.
  1547	 * It used to be dropped here, which is the exact failure this substrate
  1548	 * exists to refuse: a declared rule that quietly does not run.
  1549	 *
  1550	 * Reported through the substrate's own internal-failure branch, because
  1551	 * that is what it is — the validator could not decide something it was
  1552	 * told to decide. A declaration that considers the absence legitimate says
  1553	 * so with `whenOperandAbsent: "skip"`, in data a reviewer can see.
  1554	 */
  1555	function reportUndecided(run: Run, path: string, what: string): void {
  1556	  const branch = run.surface.substrate.internalFailure;
  1557	  run.emit(path, render(branch.message, { path, value: what }));
  1558	}
  1559	
  1560	function runDeferred(run: Run): void {
  1561	  // A membership lane deferred for a PENDING operand runs once, in the
  1562	  // order the walk queued it. The queue is drained, never re-fed: every
  1563	  // operand has completed by now, so a second pass could only loop.
  1564	  const queued = run.deferred.splice(0, run.deferred.length);
  1565	  for (const check of queued) {
  1566	    // `dependsOn` re-reads cleanly now: the answer is keyed by the citing
  1567	    // INSTANCE, so replaying a queued check later in the walk cannot pick
  1568	    // up a different instance's verdict.
  1569	    checkMembership(run, check.frame, check.rule, check.candidate, check.slots);
  1570	  }
  1571	  // What is still OWED, per instance. A sibling that failed is the trace
  1572	  // and discharges it; otherwise the config vanished unvalidated and
  1573	  // nothing else says so.
  1574	  for (const owed of run.obligations.splice(0, run.obligations.length)) {
  1575	    if (owed.dependsOn.some((tag) => !run.reliableTag(owed.frame.segments, tag))) continue;
  1576	    reportUndecided(
  1577	      run,
  1578	      owed.frame.path,
  1579	      `the delegated config could not be validated — the registry has no registration named ` +
  1580	        `${JSON.stringify(owed.by)}, so nothing checked it and it does not reach the admitted value`,
  1581	    );
  1582	  }
  1583	  for (const check of run.deferred.splice(0, run.deferred.length)) {
  1584	    if (check.rule.whenOperandAbsent === "skip") continue;
  1585	    reportUndecided(
  1586	      run,
  1587	      check.frame.path,
  1588	      `the ${check.rule.relation} lane could not be decided — its operand ` +
  1589	        `${describeSelector(check.rule.target)} names a position the walk never evaluated`,
  1590	    );
  1591	  }
  1592	}
  1593	
  1594	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1595	// Entry points.
  1596	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1597	
  1598	/**
  1599	 * Evaluate a declared `default:` against the field it defaults, with NO
  1600	 * document around it — the load-time half of "no value reaches the
  1601	 * admitted form unvalidated". A default that its own field would refuse is
  1602	 * a fault in the DECLARATION, and its author should hear about it once, at
  1603	 * load, rather than through every template that omits the key.
  1604	 *
  1605	 * The deferred queue is deliberately left undrained: a lane reading the
  1606	 * DOCUMENT cannot be decided without one, and a default that is legal in
  1607	 * one template and not another is a template's finding, not a
  1608	 * declaration's. What survives here is context-free — a wrong type, a
  1609	 * grammar, a value outside an enum.
  1610	 */
  1611	export function defaultFindings(
  1612	  surface: SurfaceDecl,
  1613	  field: NodeDecl,
  1614	  value: unknown,
  1615	): readonly ValidationFinding[] {
  1616	  const run = new Run(surface, { kind: "direct" }, {});
  1617	  run.root = undefined;
  1618	  evaluateNode(run, field, {
  1619	    at: { node: field, decl: "$" },
  1620	    path: "$",
  1621	    segments: [],
  1622	    value,
  1623	    parentValue: undefined,
  1624	  });
  1625	  return run.findings;
  1626	}
  1627	
  1628	/**
  1629	 * Run a surface declaration over a value graph. ONE computation serves
  1630	 * both channels (D1): the caller supplies `channel`, and the source-bearing
  1631	 * attributes are inert where no source exists.
  1632	 */
  1633	export function runSurface(surface: SurfaceDecl, value: unknown, opts: EngineOptions): EngineRun {
  1634	  const run = new Run(surface, opts.channel, { gateCatalog: opts.catalog });
  1635	  run.root = value;
  1636	  const rootFrame: Frame = { at: run.rootAt, path: "$", segments: [], value, parentValue: undefined };
  1637	  const rootDecl = surface.root;
  1638	
  1639	  // The root container precondition: with no map operand nothing below has
  1640	  // an address, so the ONE finding is the whole result. It applies where
  1641	  // the ROOT is declared as a map — a surface whose root is any other
  1642	  // kind evaluates through its own node like every other.
  1643	  const rootIsMap =
  1644	    rootDecl.kind === "map.fixed" || rootDecl.kind === "map.open" || rootDecl.kind === "map.plain";
  1645	  if (rootIsMap && !isContainer(value)) {
  1646	    run.emit("$", render(rootDecl.containerMessage, { path: "$", value }));
  1647	    return {
  1648	      findings: run.findings,
  1649	      normalized: undefined,
  1650	      effectiveConfigs: run.effectiveConfigs,
  1651	      runtimeContextBindings: run.runtimeContextBindings,
  1652	    };
  1653	  }
  1654	
  1655	  // The substrate's `resolve.graph: acyclic` lane. It ACCUMULATES with the
  1656	  // structural lanes (a cycle is not a container, so it suppresses
  1657	  // nothing), and it has an operand only where a resolved graph exists.
  1658	  if (run.channel.kind === "file") {
  1659	    const cycle = findCycle(value, "$", new Set());
  1660	    if (cycle !== undefined) {
  1661	      run.emit(cycle, render(surface.substrate.resolve.graph.message, { path: cycle }));
  1662	    }
  1663	  }
  1664	
  1665	  const result = evaluateNode(run, rootDecl, rootFrame);
  1666	  runDeferred(run);
  1667	  for (const rule of surface.crossRules) {
  1668	    if (rule.relation === "equals") evaluateEquals(run, rootFrame, rule);
  1669	  }
  1670	  return {
  1671	    findings: run.findings,
  1672	    normalized: result.value,
  1673	    effectiveConfigs: run.effectiveConfigs,
  1674	    runtimeContextBindings: run.runtimeContextBindings,
  1675	  };
  1676	}

  1223	/** The gates subtree's file-channel key-STRINGNESS scan: a non-string key
  1224	 * cannot become an own property and would blind every own-key scan, so it
  1225	 * reports at the NEAREST addressable path (the containing map's). */
  1226	function scanKeyStringness(
  1227	  run: Run,
  1228	  value: unknown,
  1229	  path: string,
  1230	  message: MessageTemplate,
  1231	  seen: WeakSet<object>,
  1232	): void {
  1233	  if (typeof value !== "object" || value === null) return;
  1234	  if (seen.has(value)) return;
  1235	  seen.add(value);
  1236	  if (Array.isArray(value)) {
  1237	    value.forEach((item, index) => scanKeyStringness(run, item, indexPath(path, index), message, seen));
  1238	    return;
  1239	  }
  1240	  if (!isResolvedMap(value)) return;
  1241	  for (const [key, child] of value.entries()) {
  1242	    if (typeof key !== "string") {
  1243	      run.emit(path, render(message, { path, key, value: key }));
  1244	      continue;
  1245	    }
  1246	    scanKeyStringness(run, child, joinPath(path, key), message, seen);
  1247	  }
  1248	}

  1250	function evalList(
  1251	  run: Run,
  1252	  decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "list" }>,
  1253	  frame: Frame,

  1276	      parentAt: frame.at,

  1290	    }

  1293	      // the member lane's, which `memberLaneAt` sets for a different lane.
  1294	      const at = decl.unique.at === "container" ? frame.path : indexPath(frame.path, index);

  1296	        run.emit(at, render(decl.unique.message, { ...slots, path: at, value: member }));
  1297	      }

  1299	    }
  1300	    clean.push({ member, path: memberPath });
  1301	  });

  1305	        at: memberAt,

  1311	        parentValue: value,
  1312	        ownerKey: frame.ownerKey,
  1313	      };

  1315	        ...slots,
  1316	        path: entry.path,
  1317	        value: entry.member,
  1318	      });
  1319	    }
  1320	  }
  1321	  return { ok, value: normalized };

  1324	function evalString(
  1325	  run: Run,
  1326	  decl: Extract<NodeDecl, { kind: "string" }>,
  1327	  frame: Frame,
